```
## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFjHLDDpCHZLcZ1qs9if -->

Here's the outcome.

## Is \`Interface_Static::SetIVal\` available?

**Yes.** The safe wrapper is \`opencascade_sys::interface::Static::set_i_val(name: &str, val: i32) -> bool\` (wrapping \`Interface_Static::SetIVal\` via the \`Interface_Static_set_i_val\` FFI), alongside \`set_c_val\`/\`set_r_val\` and the getters \`i_val\`/\`c_val\`/\`r_val\`. \`write_step_file\` already uses \`set_c_val\` for the schema, so the pattern was ready to go.

## Did mode = 0 work?

**No — I tried it and it doesn't fix the issue**, so I reverted it. The evidence is actually quite illuminating:

- Setting \`write.surfacecurve.mode = 0\` **did** take effect — the written STEP dropped from 20 B-spline pcurves + seam/surface curves to **zero pcurves**, leaving only the (exact) 3D edge curves.
- But the re-imported volume was **still 3087 mm³** (14% low). Adding \`read.surfacecurve.mode = 3\` (3D-preferred) didn't change it either.

That's a meaningful result: it **relocates the bug**. The degeneracy is *not* in the writer's pcurve→B-spline conversion, as I'd hypothesized — OCCT **rebuilds the pcurve from the 3D curve on read**, and *that reconstruction also degenerates* for this partial-torus periodic face. So the bug lives in OCCT 7.9.3's pcurve computation for the partial torus, hit on **both** the write and read paths. No \`surfacecurve.mode\` setting can route around it.

## Bottom line

The \`surfacecurve.mode\` lever is a dead end; the real fix is the **OCCT 8.0.0_p1 upgrade** (whose "partial torus creation handles inverted V range" fix matches this repro directly). I've recorded the failed workaround in the TODO note so the next session doesn't retry it, and noted that \`interface::Static::set_i_val\` is available if some future attempt needs it.

Working tree is clean; both experimental changes reverted (\`lib.rs\` back to its committed state). The only lasting change this turn is the corrected TODO note, folded into the step-5-debug commit. clippy clean.

One note: I amended the commit to correct the note, since the previously-committed version suggested the mode=0 workaround as promising — leaving that in would have misled the next session. If you'd rather I hadn't amended for a docs-only correction, say so and I'll adjust.
```

```
commit 79eff2a3c0ff4e7167864cbf480452b7cc24bb17
Author: Seth LaForge <sethml@ofb.net>
Date:   Wed Jul 22 07:23:51 2026 -0700

    Upgrade to OCCT 8.0.0; pivot collections to canonical NCollection names; expand coverage
    
    Upgrades the vendored OCCT to V8_0_0_p1 and reworks the binding generator to
    match OCCT 8.0's API, which now uses the canonical \`NCollection_*<T>\` templates
    directly and deprecated the old \`TColgp_*\`/\`TColStd_*\`/\`TopTools_*\` aliases.
    
    Binding generator:
    - Exclude deprecated 8.0 backward-compat alias headers automatically
      (\`exclude_deprecated_headers\`), so bindings target canonical NCollection types.
    - Auto-discover NCollection instantiations from referenced template spellings and
      generate them under canonical \`n_collection::\` names with full method sets,
      including collections of primitive scalars and \`HArray1\`/\`HSequence\` handle
      collections (\`to_handle\`, \`get\`/\`get_mut\`).
    - Handle types use the \`Handle_<C++name>\` scheme (e.g. \`Handle_Geom_Curve\`).
    - Auto-register handle-able \`NCollection_H*<T>\` instantiations (they inherit
      \`Standard_Transient\`), so \`Handle(NCollection_HArray1<T>)\` parameters resolve.
      Skipped symbols drop from ~1,680 to ~720.
    - Collection iterators implement the standard \`Iterator\` trait (usable in \`for\`
      loops and adapters, via a blanket \`Iterator for OwnedPtr<I: Iterator>\`).
    - Shorten collection overload-suffix tokens (\`imap_shape\`, \`arr1_pnt\`, \`harr1_pnt\`,
      … — dropping the hasher argument) for readable overloaded method names.
    - Fix the opencascade-sys compile breakages surfaced by 8.0: non-allocatable-class
      handling, the Rust-2024 \`gen\` keyword, the private \`BRepGraphInc_BitFlags::BlockType\`
      typedef, and the unbuilt TKDraw toolkit.
    
    High-level crate, examples, and tests:
    - Port \`opencascade\` and all in-repo examples to the canonical names and new idioms.
...
</persisted-output>
Shell cwd was reset to .
```
</details>

```
//! Build script for opencascade-sys
//!
//! This script:
//! 1. Uses pre-generated code from the \`generated/\` directory
//! 2. Compiles wrappers.cpp with cc
//!
//! To regenerate the bindings, run:
//!   DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/usr/lib cargo run -p opencascade-binding-generator -- \
//!     -I target/OCCT/include \
//!     -o crates/opencascade-sys/generated \
//!     $(cat crates/opencascade-sys/headers.txt | grep -v '^#' | grep -v '^$' | sed 's|^|target/OCCT/include/|')

use std::io::Write;
use std::path::PathBuf;

fn main() {
    let manifest_dir = PathBuf::from(std::env::var("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR").expect("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR not set"));
    let gen_dir = manifest_dir.join("generated");

    let target = std::env::var("TARGET").expect("No TARGET environment variable defined");
    let is_windows = target.to_lowercase().contains("windows");
    let is_windows_gnu = target.to_lowercase().contains("windows-gnu");

    // Detect OCCT installation
    let occt_config = OcctConfig::detect();

    println!("cargo:rustc-link-search=native={}", occt_config.library_dir.to_str().unwrap());
    if is_windows {
        println!("cargo:rustc-link-lib=dylib=user32");
    }

    // Find generated wrappers*.cpp files (may be split by toolkit)
    let mut wrapper_cpp_files: Vec<PathBuf> = std::fs::read_dir(&gen_dir)
        .expect("Failed to read generated/ directory")
        .filter_map(|entry| {
            let path = entry.ok()?.path();
            if path.extension().map_or(false, |e| e == "cpp")
                && path.file_stem().map_or(false, |s| s.to_string_lossy().starts_with("wrappers"))
            {
                Some(path)
            } else {
                None
            }
        })
        .collect();
    wrapper_cpp_files.sort();
    if wrapper_cpp_files.is_empty() {
        panic!("No generated wrappers*.cpp files found in {}. Run the binding generator first.", gen_dir.display());
    }

    // Derive OCCT libraries to link from generated wrapper file names.
    // Each wrappers_TK<name>.cpp corresponds to an OCCT library named TK<name>.
    let lib_type = if occt_config.is_dynamic { "dylib" } else { "static" };
    for cpp_file in &wrapper_cpp_files {
        if let Some(stem) = cpp_file.file_stem().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) {
            if let Some(lib_name) = stem.strip_prefix("wrappers_") {
                if lib_name.starts_with("TK") {
                    println!("cargo:rustc-link-lib={lib_type}={lib_name}");
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // Find manual wrapper .cpp files
    let manual_dir = manifest_dir.join("manual");
    let manual_cpp_files: Vec<PathBuf> = if manual_dir.exists() {
        std::fs::read_dir(&manual_dir)
            .expect("Failed to read manual/ directory")
            .filter_map(|entry| {
                let path = entry.ok()?.path();
                if path.extension().map_or(false, |e| e == "cpp") {
                    Some(path)
                } else {
                    None
                }
            })
            .collect()
    } else {
        Vec::new()
    };

    // Combine all per-toolkit wrappers into a single compilation unit.
    // This reduces total CPU time by avoiding redundant OCCT header parsing
    // across 50 separate files.
    let out_dir = PathBuf::from(std::env::var("OUT_DIR").expect("OUT_DIR not set"));
    let combined_cpp = out_dir.join("wrappers_combined.cpp");
    {
        let mut f = std::fs::File::create(&combined_cpp)
            .expect("Failed to create combined wrapper file");
        writeln!(f, "// Auto-generated: combines all per-toolkit wrappers into one TU").unwrap();
        for cpp_file in &wrapper_cpp_files {
            let abs = std::fs::canonicalize(cpp_file)
                .unwrap_or_else(|_| cpp_file.clone());
            writeln!(f, "#include \"{}\"", abs.display()).unwrap();
        }
    }

    // Build with cc
    let mut build = cc::Build::new();
    build.file(&combined_cpp);
    for cpp_file in &manual_cpp_files {
        build.file(cpp_file);
    }

    if is_windows_gnu {
        build.define("OCC_CONVERT_SIGNALS", "TRUE");
    }

    build
        .cpp(true)
        .flag_if_supported("-std=c++17")
        // Generated wrappers use extern "C" functions that return C++ reference types
        // (e.g. const TopoDS_Shape&). This is technically incompatible with C linkage
        // but works fine for Rust FFI where both sides agree on calling convention.
        .flag_if_supported("-Wno-unused-function")
        .flag_if_supported("-Wno-deprecated-declarations")
        .flag_if_supported("-Wno-return-type-c-linkage")
        // OCCT classes math_FunctionSample, Poly_MakeLoops, Poly_MakeLoops2D,
        // and Poly_MakeLoops3D have virtual functions but non-virtual destructors.
        // Our generated destructors always delete through the concrete type pointer
        // (e.g. delete static_cast<Poly_MakeLoops3D*>(ptr)), never through a base
        // pointer, so the non-virtual destructor is safe. OwnedPtr<T> ensures the
        // static type always matches the dynamic type.
        .flag_if_supported("-Wno-delete-non-abstract-non-virtual-dtor")
        .flag_if_supported("-Wno-delete-abstract-non-virtual-dtor")
        .define("_USE_MATH_DEFINES", "TRUE")
        .include(&gen_dir)
        .debug(false);

    // Treat the OCCT include directory as a system header path so the compiler
    // suppresses warnings from third-party OCCT code (e.g. sprintf deprecation).
    // MSVC does not support -isystem; on GCC/Clang it is standard.
    if target.contains("msvc") {
        build.include(&occt_config.include_dir);
        if let Some(ref bi) = occt_config.build_include_dir {
            build.include(bi);
        }
    } else {
        build.flag("-isystem");
        build.flag(occt_config.include_dir.to_str().unwrap());
        if let Some(bi) = &occt_config.build_include_dir {
            build.flag("-isystem");
            build.flag(bi.to_str().unwrap());
        }
    }

    build.compile("opencascade_sys_wrapper");

    println!("cargo:rustc-link-lib=static=opencascade_sys_wrapper");

    // Rerun if generated or manual files change
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=generated");
    for cpp_file in &wrapper_cpp_files {
        println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={}", cpp_file.display());
    }
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=manual");
    for cpp_file in &manual_cpp_files {
        println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={}", cpp_file.display());
    }
}

struct OcctConfig {
    include_dir: PathBuf,
    /// Optional build-time include directory containing generated \`.pxx\` headers
    /// that are not part of the installed public headers.
    build_include_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
    library_dir: PathBuf,
    is_dynamic: bool,
}

impl OcctConfig {
    /// Find OpenCASCADE library using cmake
    fn detect() -> Self {
        println!("cargo:rerun-if-env-changed=DEP_OCCT_ROOT");

        // Add path to builtin OCCT
        #[cfg(feature = "builtin")]
        {
            let builtin_occt_path = occt_sys::occt_path();

            // Skip building if OCCT appears to be already installed
            // Check for a key library file to determine if build is needed
            let marker_lib = builtin_occt_path.join("lib").join("libTKernel.a");
            if !marker_lib.exists() {
                occt_sys::build_occt();
            }
            // SAFETY: This build script is single-threaded, so there are no
            // concurrent reads of the environment variable.
            unsafe { std::env::set_var("DEP_OCCT_ROOT", builtin_occt_path.as_os_str()) };

            // With builtin feature, skip cmake detection and use built OCCT directly
            // Prefer include/ (installed headers, complete set) over build/include
            let include_dir = if builtin_occt_path.join("include").exists() {
                builtin_occt_path.join("include")
            } else if builtin_occt_path.join("build").join("include").exists() {
                builtin_occt_path.join("build").join("include")
            } else {
                panic!("Builtin OCCT include directory not found at {:?}", builtin_occt_path);
            };

            let library_dir = if builtin_occt_path.join("lib").exists() {
                builtin_occt_path.join("lib")
            } else if builtin_occt_path.join("build").join("lib").exists() {
                builtin_occt_path.join("build").join("lib")
            } else {
                panic!("Builtin OCCT library directory not found at {:?}", builtin_occt_path);
            };

                        let build_include_dir = {
                let p = builtin_occt_path.join("build").join("include");
                if p.exists() { Some(p) } else { None }
            };
            return Self {
                include_dir,
                build_include_dir,
                library_dir,
                is_dynamic: false  // Built OCCT is static
            };
        }

        // Non-builtin: use cmake detection
        #[cfg(not(feature = "builtin"))]
        {
            /// Minimum compatible version of OpenCASCADE library (major, minor)
            const OCCT_VERSION: (u8, u8) = (7, 8);

            let dst =
                std::panic::catch_unwind(|| cmake::Config::new("OCCT").register_dep("occt").build());
            let dst = dst.expect("Pre-installed OpenCASCADE library not found. You can use \`builtin\` feature if you do not want to install OCCT libraries system-wide.");

            let cfg = std::fs::read_to_string(dst.join("share").join("occ_info.txt"))
                .expect("Something went wrong when detecting OpenCASCADE library.");

            let mut version_major: Option<u8> = None;
            let mut version_minor: Option<u8> = None;
            let mut include_dir: Option<PathBuf> = None;
            let mut library_dir: Option<PathBuf> = None;
            let mut is_dynamic: bool = false;

            for line in cfg.lines() {
                if let Some((var, val)) = line.split_once('=') {
                    match var {
                        "VERSION_MAJOR" => version_major = val.parse().ok(),
                        "VERSION_MINOR" => version_minor = val.parse().ok(),
                        "INCLUDE_DIR" => include_dir = val.parse().ok(),
                        "LIBRARY_DIR" => library_dir = val.parse().ok(),
                        "BUILD_SHARED_LIBS" => is_dynamic = val == "ON",
                        _ => (),
                    }
                }
            }

            if let (Some(version_major), Some(version_minor), Some(include_dir), Some(library_dir)) =
                (version_major, version_minor, include_dir, library_dir)
            {
                if version_major != OCCT_VERSION.0 || version_minor < OCCT_VERSION.1 {
                    panic!("Pre-installed OpenCASCADE library found but version is not met (found {}.{} but {}.{} required). Please provide required version or use \`builtin\` feature.",
                           version_major, version_minor, OCCT_VERSION.0, OCCT_VERSION.1);
                }

                Self { include_dir, build_include_dir: None, library_dir, is_dynamic }
            } else {
                panic!("OpenCASCADE library found but something wrong with config.");
            }
        }
    }
}
```

```
use std::{
    env::var,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
};

const LIB_DIR: &str = "lib";
const INCLUDE_DIR: &str = "include";

/// Get the path to the OCCT library installation directory to be
/// used in build scripts.
///
/// Only valid during build (\`cargo clean\` removes these files).
pub fn occt_path() -> PathBuf {
    // moves the output into target/TARGET/OCCT
    // this way its less likely to be rebuilt without a cargo clean
    Path::new(&var("OUT_DIR").expect("missing OUT_DIR")).join("../../../../OCCT")
}

/// Build the OCCT library.
pub fn build_occt() {
    cmake::Config::new(Path::new(env!("OCCT_SRC_DIR")))
        .define("BUILD_LIBRARY_TYPE", "Static")
        .define("BUILD_MODULE_ApplicationFramework", "FALSE")
        .define("BUILD_MODULE_Draw", "FALSE")

        .profile("Release")
        .out_dir(occt_path())
        .build();
}
```

<!-- msg_011CdHHiGvhoFeMRwxbHKTxx -->

You didn't skip a step in the upgrade itself — the confusion is that brepper keeps its **own** OCCT build, separate from opencascade-rs's.

Here's the mechanism:

- brepper depends on `opencascade-sys` with the default `builtin` feature.
- `opencascade-sys`'s build script calls `occt_sys::occt_path()`, which resolves to **`brepper/target/OCCT`** (relative to brepper's `OUT_DIR`) — *not* opencascade-rs's `target/OCCT`.
- On build, it checks one marker: does `brepper/target/OCCT/lib/libTKernel.a` exist? If yes, it **skips** building OCCT entirely and just reuses whatever headers/libs are there. If no, it CMake-builds the vendored submodule from source into `brepper/target/OCCT`.

So what happened: brepper's `target/OCCT` is from **Feb 24 (OCCT 7.9.3)**. When you bumped the submodule to 8.0 in opencascade-rs, the marker lib in brepper's tree still existed, so brepper's build script never rebuilt it. Then it tried to compile the newly-generated 8.0 wrappers (which `#include <BRepGraph_ItemId.hxx>`, a new 8.0 header) against the stale 7.9.3 include dir → "file not found."

The build system has no dependency edge from "OCCT submodule pointer changed" to "rebuild OCCT" — it only checks the marker file. So the correct way to rebuild is to **remove the stale marker** and let it rebuild from source:

- **Clean rebuild (the honest/correct path):** `rm -rf target/OCCT` (or `cargo clean`), then `cargo build`. This CMake-builds OCCT 8.0 from the submodule — correct, but ~15 min.
- **Shortcut (what I was attempting):** copy opencascade-rs's already-built `target/OCCT` (fresh 8.0, built Jul 21) into brepper's tree. Same static config, relocatable — saves the rebuild, but reuses artifacts built elsewhere.

Which would you prefer? I lean toward the clean from-source rebuild now that we're at a fresh OCCT version — it's slower but removes any doubt about mismatched artifacts.

<!-- msg_011CdHHoBRTHuijRWBLHSDRT -->

It's serviceable but it has two real design smells — and notably, both live in **opencascade-rs**, not in brepper. The `path = "../opencascade-rs/..."` dependency itself is fine and normal for two crates you co-develop.

**Smell 1 — the footgun we just hit: marker-only invalidation.** `occt-sys`'s builtin build gates entirely on "does `libTKernel.a` exist?" There's no record of *which* OCCT the existing build corresponds to, and no `cargo:rerun-if-changed` tied to the submodule. So bumping the submodule SHA silently leaves every consumer's `target/OCCT` stale until someone manually `rm -rf`s it. That's not a brepper problem to work around — it's a missing invalidation in `occt_sys::build_occt`.

The clean fix, in `occt-sys`:
- Write a stamp file (e.g. `target/OCCT/.occt-sha`) containing the submodule's checked-out SHA (or `git describe`) after a successful build.
- Gate the rebuild on **stamp-matches-current-SHA**, not just marker existence.
- Emit `cargo:rerun-if-changed` on the submodule ref so cargo re-runs the build script when it moves.

That alone would have turned today's silent breakage into an automatic rebuild.

**Smell 2 — every consumer builds its own OCCT.** Because `occt_path()` derives from the consumer's `OUT_DIR`, brepper and opencascade-rs each keep a separate ~hundreds-of-MB, ~15-min OCCT build. My "copy the sibling's build" shortcut was really me hand-doing what the build system should do itself. Cleaner options, roughly in order of effort:
- **Shared cache keyed by SHA:** build OCCT once into a stable, SHA-keyed cache dir (e.g. `~/.cache/occt/<sha>/`) and symlink/point consumers at it. Sibling crates on the same OCCT reuse one build.
- **Env override:** honor something like `OCCT_PREBUILT_ROOT` in the builtin path so you can point a workspace at an existing tree explicitly (you already have `DEP_OCCT_ROOT` plumbing for the non-builtin path).

My honest recommendation: **fix Smell 1 in opencascade-rs** (SHA-stamped invalidation) — it's small, and it's the thing that actually bit us. Smell 2 is a nice-to-have. The brepper→opencascade-rs path dep needs no change.

2. Detour into opencascade-rs first to add the SHA-stamp invalidation, then let brepper rebuild through the fixed path?

**After:**
```
use std::{
    env::var,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
};

const LIB_DIR: &str = "lib";
const INCLUDE_DIR: &str = "include";

/// Name of the stamp file (written under [`occt_path`]) recording which OCCT
/// source revision the built artifacts correspond to. See [`occt_source_stamp`].
const STAMP_FILE: &str = ".occt-src-stamp";

/// Get the path to the OCCT library installation directory to be
/// used in build scripts.
///
/// Only valid during build (`cargo clean` removes these files).
pub fn occt_path() -> PathBuf {
    // moves the output into target/TARGET/OCCT
    // this way its less likely to be rebuilt without a cargo clean
    Path::new(&var("OUT_DIR").expect("missing OUT_DIR")).join("../../../../OCCT")
}

/// A short identifier for the OCCT source the current bindings target.
///
/// This is the checked-out commit of the vendored OCCT submodule. It is written
/// into a stamp file after a successful build (see [`build_occt`]) and compared
/// on subsequent builds so that bumping the submodule (e.g. a version upgrade)
/// invalidates a stale prebuilt tree instead of being silently reused. Falls
/// back to the empty string if git is unavailable, which degrades to the old
/// "marker file exists" behaviour.
pub fn occt_source_stamp() -> String {
    let src = env!("OCCT_SRC_DIR");
    std::process::Command::new("git")
        .args(["-C", src, "rev-parse", "HEAD"])
        .output()
        .ok()
        .filter(|out| out.status.success())
        .map(|out| String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Whether the prebuilt OCCT under [`occt_path`] is present and matches the
/// current source revision, so a rebuild can be skipped.
pub fn occt_is_up_to_date() -> bool {
    let path = occt_path();
    let marker_lib = path.join(LIB_DIR).join("libTKernel.a");
    if !marker_lib.exists() {
        return false;
    }
    match std::fs::read_to_string(path.join(STAMP_FILE)) {
        Ok(stamp) => stamp.trim() == occt_source_stamp(),
        // Marker present but no stamp: an older or externally-produced build we
        // can't verify — rebuild to be safe.
        Err(_) => false,
    }
}
```
</details>

```
fn write_tessellated_stl(shape: &topo_ds::Shape, dir: &str, name: &str, deflection: f64, angle_deg: f64) {
    let _mesh = b_rep_mesh::IncrementalMesh::new_shape_real_bool_real_bool(
        shape, deflection, false, angle_deg.to_radians(), false,
    );
    let stl_path = format!("{dir}/{name}.stl");
    let mut writer = stl_api::Writer::new();
    *writer.ascii_mode() = false;
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    if !writer.write(shape, &stl_path, &progress) {
        panic!("failed to write {stl_path}");
    }
    let (uknots, umults) = clamped_cubic_knots(n);
    let (vknots, vmults) = clamped_cubic_knots(n);
    let surf = geom::BSplineSurface::new_array2ofpnt_array1ofreal2_array1ofinteger2_int2(
        &poles, &uknots, &vknots, &umults, &vmults, 3, 3,
    );
    geom::BSplineSurface::to_handle(surf)
}

/// Exact cubic B-spline curve in the XZ plane (y=0) from poles at
/// x = x0 + i*d, z from \`zs\`.
fn profile_bspline_curve(x0: f64, d: f64, zs: &[f64]) -> OwnedPtr<geom::Handle_Geom_BSplineCurve> {
    let n = zs.len();
    let mut poles = n_collection::Array1_gp_Pnt::new_int2(1, n as i32);
    for (i, z) in zs.iter().enumerate() {
        let p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(x0 + i as f64 * d, 0.0, *z);
        poles.set_value(i as i32 + 1, &p);
    }
    let (knots, mults) = clamped_cubic_knots(n);
    let curve = geom::BSplineCurve::new_array1ofpnt_array1ofreal_array1ofinteger_int(
        &poles, &knots, &mults, 3,
    );
    geom::BSplineCurve::to_handle(curve)
}
```

```
1863:pub fn stage2(
pub fn stage2(
    config: &Config,
    mesh: ConnectedMesh,
    viz: Option<&crate::viz::VizSender>,
) -> Result<Stage2Output, Stage2Error> {
    // --- Stage 2.1(a): sharp-edge classification (fit-free) ---
    let t = Instant::now();
    let edge_classes = edges::classify_edges(&mesh, config.angular_tolerance_rad);
    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        let total = edge_classes.sharp_count + edge_classes.interior_count;
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.1(a) ({:.3}s): Sharp-edge classification: {} manifold edges — {} sharp, {} interior",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            total,
            edge_classes.sharp_count,
            edge_classes.interior_count,
        );
    }
    // --- Stage 2.1(b): osculating-jet curvature prior ---
    let t = Instant::now();
    let jet = edges::compute_jet_field(&mesh, &edge_classes);
    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.1(b) ({:.3}s): Jet curvature prior: {} faces summarized",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            jet.len(),
        );
    }
    let mut segmentation = Segmentation {
        edge_classes,
        jet,
        face_region: Vec::new(),
        regions: Vec::new(),
    };
    if config.viz_active(2, 1) {
        if let Some(vs) = viz {
            let overlay = segmentation_edges_overlay(&mesh, &segmentation.edge_classes);
            if matches!(vs.show_and_wait(overlay), VizAction::Quit) {
                return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));
            }
        }
    }
    if !config.stage.at_least(2, 2) {
        return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));
    }

    // --- Stage 2.2: region extraction ---
    let t = Instant::now();
    let (face_region, region_list) =
        regions::extract_regions(&mesh, &segmentation.edge_classes);
    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        let single = region_list.iter().filter(|r| r.faces.len() == 1).count();
        let largest = region_list.iter().map(|r| r.faces.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.2 ({:.3}s): Region extraction: {} regions over {} faces ({} single-face, largest {} faces)",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            region_list.len(),
            mesh.faces.len(),
            single,
            largest,
        );
        if config.verbosity >= 2 {
            for (i, r) in region_list.iter().enumerate() {
                if r.faces.len() > 1 {
                    eprintln!(
                        "  Region {}: {} faces, {} vertices (first face {})",
                        i,
                        r.faces.len(),
                        r.vertices.len(),
                        r.faces[0],
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }
    segmentation.face_region = face_region;
    segmentation.regions = region_list;
    if config.viz_active(2, 2) {
        if let Some(vs) = viz {
            let overlay = segmentation_regions_overlay(&mesh, &segmentation);
            if matches!(vs.show_and_wait(overlay), VizAction::Quit) {
                return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));
            }
        }
    }

    if !config.stage.at_least(2, 3) {
        return Ok(segmentation_only_output(mesh, segmentation));
    }

    // --- Stage 2.3: per-region surface discovery (ladder + competition) ---
    let t = Instant::now();
    let mut cls = classify::classify_regions(&mesh, &segmentation.regions, &segmentation.jet, config);
    if config.verbosity >= 1 {
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.3 ({:.3}s): Region classification: {} surfaces ({} planar, {} cyl/cone, {} spherical, {} toroidal)",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            cls.selected.len(),
            cls.planar.len(),
            cls.conical.len(),
            cls.spherical.len(),
            cls.toroidal.len(),
        );
    }

    // --- Stage 2.4: resolution & repair (relabel, merge, re-split) ---
    if config.stage.at_least(2, 4) {
        let t = Instant::now();
        let stats = repair::repair(&mesh, &mut cls, config);
        if config.verbosity >= 1 {
            eprintln!(
                "Stage 2.4 ({:.3}s): Repair: {} relabeled, {} merged, {} re-split → {} surfaces",
                t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
                stats.relabeled,
                stats.merged,
                stats.split,
                cls.selected.len(),
            );
        }
    }
```

```
## Original handoff (what to test + likely failure points)

## What was built (compete.rs)

Torus is now a competition candidate — NOT the kickoff's medial-spine/k=1/R
design (those stayed contingency; the existing 7-DOF \`fit_torus\` sufficed):

1. **Direct candidate**: every curved seed with \`|κ2| < 0.8|κ1|\` also runs
   \`grow::<ToroidalSurface>\` (sphere-like seeds skipped — sphere quad-row
   bands lie near tori). Recognizes fillet tori generally, incl. next to
   cones (promotion only triggers on cylinders).
2. **Bent-tube promotion** (\`promote_bend\`): a grown cylinder's frontier is
   scanned for tube-continuation jet (|κ1| within 2× of 1/r, |κ2| ≤ 0.8|κ1|);
   a torus is fitted to the pure-bend patch past the stall line (frontier +
   off-cylinder tube-jet neighbours, depth ≤ 4, cap 64; flood starts
   deepest-off-cylinder first, ≤ 4 tries). Key: the cylinder fit locates the
   tangency, so the patch can't straddle it (jet class can't tell bend from
   straight — same κ1). Result competes as a normal candidate (dof 3).
3. Guard: reject torus fits with \`major_radius > bbox diagonal\` (straight
   segments fit absurdly distant tori). \`grow\` split into \`grow\`+\`grow_from\`.

Smoke: pipe_elbow_10_medium → 2 planes + 2 cyl + 1 torus, 1 valid solid, 0
leftovers (the acceptance shape, first run — but --compare NOT run).
\`stage2_competition\` (2 tests) green. Nothing else verified.

## What to test

- \`pipe_elbow_10_medium\` + \`_fine\` with \`--compare\` vs
  tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10.step (volume gate, BRepCheck).
- stepped_block_toroidal_*, cone_cylinder (concave fillet, jet k2<0),
  filleted_cylinder/pipe/hole_block, rounded_cube_* (esp. coarse — blob
  fallback interaction), pill.
- Full suites vs baseline: stage2 130 / stage3 99 / stage4 27, lib 90, seg
  14. Torus models previously fell back to valid per-facet → no regression
  pressure, a clean bar. Add stage2_competition tests for the new wins.

## Likely failure points (ranked)

- **κ2 gates (0.8, two places)**: inner-bend saddle reads |κ2| =
  1/(R−r) → up to κ1·r/(R−r); tight bends (R−r small) get excluded from
  direct seeding AND the bend patch. Loosen/rethink if inner-bend faces are
  missing from claims.
- **Direct-candidate seed_patch straddle**: for fillet tori tangent to
  cones/cylinders, the jet-similar patch (class Developable, same κ1) can
  straddle → contaminated fit → NO GROWTH → blob fallback per-facets the
  region (pre-step-5 behaviour, not a regression, but no win). Fix would be
  a cone-side promotion or patch tightening.
- **Coarse meshes**: GROW_VTOL_FACTOR=30 may reject coarse bend rows
  (rounded_cube_coarse precedent) → leftover blob → per-facet fallback.
- **Genuine strip sweeps**: the bend of pipe_elbow is gridded, but a
  strip-only tessellated sweep will defeat \`fit_torus\` (needs both
  curvature directions in the patch) → that is the trigger to implement the
  plan's k=1/R degenerate-limit model (§2.3(c)), not to tune thresholds.
- **Sphere-band tori**: if a bogus torus ever beats a sphere, competition
  lacks toroidal.rs's \`angular_coverage_valid\`/\`minor_circle_coverage_valid\`
  (only the bbox-R guard is applied) — wire those in rather than masking.
- **Convexity**: TorusFit convex comes from the first accepted face; flood
  rejects opposite-convexity faces. Pipe exterior is uniformly convex around
  the tube; watch concave fillets.
- **Runtime**: every curved non-sphere-like seed now runs an LM torus fit
  (+ up to 4 refits per promotion). If large models crawl, gate harder.

Debug: \`BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE=1\` logs per-commit, NO-GROWTH (now with torus
```

## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Bash: Commit opencascade-rs stamp fix</summary>

```bash
cd /Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs
cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sethml-src-brepper/b3de8516-c627-417f-be5b-353987c7c589/scratchpad/occt_msg.txt <<'EOF'
occt-sys: rebuild builtin OCCT when the source revision changes

The builtin OCCT build gated only on "does libTKernel.a exist?", so bumping
the vendored submodule (e.g. the 8.0 upgrade) silently reused a stale prebuilt
tree in each consumer's target/OCCT until a manual `rm -rf`. It surfaced as
`BRepGraph_ItemId.hxx file not found` when freshly generated 8.0 wrappers were
compiled against stale 7.9.3 headers.

Record the submodule's checked-out commit in a `.occt-src-stamp` file after
each successful build, and skip rebuilding only when the marker library is
present AND the stamp matches the current source (`occt_is_up_to_date`). A
marker present without a matching stamp is treated as stale and rebuilt.
Degrades to the old marker-only behaviour when git is unavailable.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git add crates/occt-sys/src/lib.rs crates/opencascade-sys/build.rs
git commit -F /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sethml-src-brepper/b3de8516-c627-417f-be5b-353987c7c589/scratchpad/occt_msg.txt 2>&1 | tail -3
```

<!-- msg_011CdHhLqEq8A3eG7rgSk6TH -->

Done. Here's the summary.

## What happened

**1. Why the build was broken.** brepper keeps its *own* builtin OCCT under `brepper/target/OCCT` (separate from opencascade-rs's), and the build gated only on "does `libTKernel.a` exist?" — so your submodule bump to 8.0 was silently ignored and the freshly-generated 8.0 wrappers were compiled against stale 7.9.3 headers → `BRepGraph_ItemId.hxx not found`. You didn't skip a step; the build system had no invalidation edge for a source-revision change.

**2. Fixed the invalidation (opencascade-rs, commit `a9bf1bf` on `rust-2024`).** `occt-sys` now writes a `.occt-src-stamp` (the submodule's checked-out SHA) after each build and rebuilds when it mismatches (`occt_is_up_to_date`), instead of the marker-only check. Per your instruction, `brepper/target/OCCT` is now a **symlink** to `../opencascade-rs/target/OCCT` (its fresh 8.0 build) with a matching stamp — sharing one build rather than duplicating the ~15-min compile.

**3. Ported brepper to the 8.0 API (commit `4b20904` on `experiment-fable`).** 5 files: `t_colgp::/t_col_std::` collection types → `n_collection::`, `HandleGeom*` → `geom::Handle_Geom_*`, and shortened ctor/method names. A recurring trap: rustc's fuzzy "similar name" suggestions kept pointing at the wrong *rational* BSpline overloads — I verified each arg list and used the correct non-rational ones.

**4. Tests: zero regressions.** Results match the documented pre-upgrade baseline *exactly* — lib 90, stage1 83, stage2_segmentation 14, stage2_competition all green, stage2_integration 132/53 (stale pre-rewrite suite), stage3 99/7, stage4 28/8, same failing set. All remaining failures are pre-existing and documented (stale BFS-era tests, coarse-mesh blob fallback, ccad geometry mismatch).

**5. The STEP round-trip issue is FIXED.** pipe_elbow_10_fine's written STEP now reads back at the correct volume 3589.9 mm³ (round-trip rel diff 7.6e-9) and passes `--compare` end-to-end — it was a 14% loss (3087 mm³) under 7.9.3. OCCT 8.0.0_p1's partial-torus fix resolved it with no brepper code change, exactly as hypothesized.

One incidental note worth flagging: OCCT 8.0's STEP reader now *loads* the 4 ccad reference STEPs that gave "Reterror" under 7.9.3. Those tests still fail, but now at a stage-3 geometry compare (~3.95 mm edge distance) — a pre-existing reconstruction mismatch that was previously masked by the load failure, not a regression.

Memory updated (project-state + step-5-debug + index). The only uncommitted items are your untracked `tests/downloaded/` STL/STEP files, which I left alone.

```
DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:532:3. **Resample a grid** through the flattened triangulation (uniform-bin point location; numeric misses near corner slivers are clamped to the nearest triangle within one bin — feeding them to the hole membrane extrapolates garbage). Grid stations per axis are the boundary vertices' own pinned positions (stations *between* the vertices of a coarse profile sample chord interiors and pin the fit to the chordal prism — a 1.7% volume deficit on the arch, the mesh's own chordal error), subdivided up to an interior-density target of ~1.5·√(region vertices) (a flat margin tessellates coarsely: hill_with_hole has 8-vertex sides on a 946-vertex region). Station spacing is **conditioned in 3D-arc measure** (\`axis_stations\`): chord-length-parametrized C2 interpolation amplifies data noise by roughly the square of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio, so a boundary mixing fine facets with one long straight chord (the xyz-cube letter walls: a 21× jump) turned a 0.02 mm-flat ribbon into control points 150 mm off the part while passing through every grid node exactly. Near-coincident stations (< 0.25× the median arc gap — the near-singular end of the same conditioning problem) are merged, and segments are subdivided to a fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor segment's pieces, uniformly in arc rather than UV (the Floater map compresses high-curvature areas). Surviving boundary-vertex stations keep their exact pinned positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat and mid-segment samples lie on the true surface. Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`.
DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md:534:5. **Validate**: first a **control-point bound check** — every pole must lie within the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal. An ill-conditioned fit passes through every grid node exactly yet swings wildly between them, and the vertex/centroid validation below samples at data points, making it structurally blind to inter-node excursions; the surface lies in the convex hull of its poles, so poles near the data bound the whole surface near the data (the xyz-cube blow-up put poles 22 diagonals out; legit fits, including periodic bands, stay well inside the margin). Then every region vertex and face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fit, each projection seeded from the vertex's own parameterization value (sequential seeding breaks at mesh row wraps, and neither \`NextValueOfUV\` nor \`ValueOfUV\` recovers reliably on many-knot surfaces). On failure at every grid density, the region returns to the 2.4 fallback ladder.
LEARNINGS.md:538:Chord-length-parametrized C2 cubic interpolation is ill-conditioned when adjacent data spacing jumps sharply: noise amplification grows roughly with the SQUARE of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio. On the xyz-cube letter walls (grid stations pinned to boundary vertices mixing 0.175 mm facet chords with one 3.66 mm straight chord — a 21× jump), a ribbon flat to 0.02 mm interpolated to a surface with control points 150 mm off the part — while \`IsDone()\` returned true, the residual at every grid node was ~1e-14, and per-vertex/per-centroid validation passed (all those sample at or near data points; the excursion lives BETWEEN nodes). Two defenses in stage 2.7: \`axis_stations\` conditions station spacing in 3D-arc measure (merge stations closer than 0.25× the median gap; subdivide, uniformly in arc, until no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor's pieces), and \`fit_grid\` rejects any fit with a control point outside the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal (the surface lies in its poles' convex hull, so poles near the data bound the surface near the data — legit fits, including periodic bands, stay well inside).
```

<!-- msg_011CdHiYo1qPtmDXu1xkMQN9 -->

Good question to push on — the answer is **no**, nothing in the automated suite runs on it.

- **No test loads `tests/downloaded/3DBenchy.stl` or `.step`.** The only place the file path appears is a manual repro command in `TODO.md:135` (`./target/release/brepper tests/downloaded/3DBenchy.stl -o /tmp/benchy.step`). The `#[test]` functions in `stage3.rs` that mention "3DBenchy" are regression tests that **inline the minimal failing geometry** (e.g. `bounded_bspline_trim_parameters_stay_in_domain` builds a small hardcoded point array reproducing the crash shape) — they don't read the 11 MB file. That's deliberate: full stage 2 on 3DBenchy's ~196k faces ran ~15 min under the old pipeline, far too slow for the suite.

- So 3DBenchy is a **committed manual-corpus asset**: used for hand-run debugging and as the historical origin of many regression comments, but not exercised by `cargo test`.

That reframes your untracked files: `OrcaCube v2` and `xyz-cube-test-20mm` are the same *kind* of thing 3DBenchy is — manual corpus models, not test inputs. So "commit them like 3DBenchy" would mean committing them purely as manual-run assets, not because any test needs them (none does, and `xyz-cube` is only referenced conceptually in comments).

Given that, the real question is whether you want ~3.2 MB of manual-only models in the repo history. Options:
1. **Commit** them to `tests/downloaded/` as manual corpus (matches 3DBenchy precedent).
2. **Leave untracked** (they stay local; fine if they're scratch).
3. **Gitignore** `tests/downloaded/` for un-referenced downloads while keeping the two 3DBenchy files tracked — though that's a bit inconsistent.

I'd lean toward (1) only if you actually reach for these in manual runs the way you do 3DBenchy; otherwise (2). Which do you want?
